# Unit 9 linguistic and owned-edition mapping packet

This packet prepares review. It records an unsigned structural mapping proposal. It does not record a qualified review, an approved edition alignment, or an install decision.

The owned-edition mapping worksheet separates observed edition facts, the proposed lesson and printed page range, unresolved edition questions, and matters that still require qualified linguistic review. ISBN, owned-copy verification, variance, reviewer identity, qualification, date, and approval stay blank. The disposition remains PENDING_CURRICULUM_REVIEWER.

Unit 9 remains in development. Its readiness is content-complete and review-pending. The Lesson 9 relationship is a proposed, unsigned mapping. It is not an approved edition alignment.

`mappingPackageDigest` binds the proposed edition-mapping facts for Unit 9: edition identity, lesson, printed page range, topic description, observation source, ISBN observation state, and the pending disposition. Reviewer identity, qualification, date, notes, correction notes, and signature stay outside that digest.

`packContentReviewDigest` is the repository aggregate of instructional curriculum. It is not an approval of this unit, and it is not part of the review-package digest.

`reviewPackageDigest` identifies this complete pending review package. It binds the ordered instructional records and the mapping-package digest. Changing a proposed page range or topic changes the mapping-package digest and therefore this package digest.

`repositorySourceDigest` binds repository and release inputs. Recording an approval later changes that repository hash. That change does not replace the signed content-review digest or the mapping-package digest.

The marker scan used by this package detects only a fixed list of known prohibited mapping-input markers. It is not comprehensive copyright detection and cannot recognize every copy or close paraphrase of protected material.

recordType=pattern
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Numbers?oldid=4371358
sourceRevision=4371358
sourceRecordId=numbers:If the item needs to be in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, use the 
originalSourceText=If the item needs to be in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, use the following rule.  

The numeral один (1) should be used in the proper form to indicate gender (and, number, believe it or not).  For example: один мальчик (one boy), одна девушка (one girl), одно животное (one animal), одни джинсы (one [pair of] jeans).  The plural form of the numeral (1) is a bit of an oddity, but its use will become clear with time.  Following a form of один should come the item at hand in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, as appropriate.

The numerals два (or две), три, and четыре all require the following item to be presented in the genitive singular form.  The numeral два has two forms: два for masculine and neuter items and две for feminine items.  After the numeral два, each numeral has only a single form.  See for example:  две девушки (two girls), три кошки (three cats), четыре мальчика (four boys).  

The numerals пять (5) through двадцать (20) require their attached items in the genitive plural forms, as in these examples:  пять девушек (five girls), десять копеек (ten kopeks), шестнадцать рублей (sixteen rubles).  

Numerals larger than twenty follow the above rules using the last digit.  Think of numerals ending in zero as ending in ten.  For example:  тридцать три рюмки (thirty-three shot glasses), сорок один год (forty-one years), шестьдесят девять слов (sixty-nine words).  

Numerals larger than 99 follow the above rules using the last two digits. For example: двести три собаки (two hundred and three dogs), двести тринадцать собак (two hundred and thirteen dogs).

When the items at hand belong not in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, the numerals themselves must be declined in addition to the declension of the items following them.  This topic shall be discussed here later.  For now, be glad that these forms are relatively uncommon.  Examples follow:  Я видел двух друзей (I saw two friends), Ты не съела ни одной штуки (You didn't eat a single piece).

In short, for nominative (and inanimate accusative) items, use the following rule to select the proper form of the item:

1 -> nominative

2, 3, 4 -> genitive singular

5 and more -> genitive plural

===A few more examples===
* один час
* два часа
* пять часов 
* двадцать один час
* двадцать два часа
shippedText=1 -> nominative
transformation=selection

The numerals два (or две), три, and четыре all require the following item to be presented in the genitive singular form.  The numeral два has two forms: два for masculine and neuter items and две for feminine items.  After the numeral два, each numeral has only a single form.  See for example:  две девушки (two girls), три кошки (three cats), четыре мальчика (four boys).  

The numerals пять (5) through двадцать (20) require their attached items in the genitive plural forms, as in these examples:  пять девушек (five girls), десять копеек (ten kopeks), шестнадцать рублей (sixteen rubles).  

Numerals larger than twenty follow the above rules using the last digit.  Think of numerals ending in zero as ending in ten.  For example:  тридцать три рюмки (thirty-three shot glasses), сорок один год (forty-one years), шестьдесят девять слов (sixty-nine words).  

Numerals larger than 99 follow the above rules using the last two digits. For example: двести три собаки (two hundred and three dogs), двести тринадцать собак (two hundred and thirteen dogs).

When the items at hand belong not in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, the numerals themselves must be declined in addition to the declension of the items following them.  This topic shall be discussed here later.  For now, be glad that these forms are relatively uncommon.  Examples follow:  Я видел двух друзей (I saw two friends), Ты не съела ни одной штуки (You didn't eat a single piece).

In short, for nominative (and inanimate accusative) items, use the following rule to select the proper form of the item:

1 -> nominative

2, 3, 4 -> genitive singular

5 and more -> genitive plural

===A few more examples===
* один час
* два часа
* пять часов 
* двадцать один час
* двадцать два часа

recordType=pattern
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Numbers?oldid=4371358
sourceRevision=4371358
sourceRecordId=numbers:If the item needs to be in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, use the 
originalSourceText=If the item needs to be in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, use the following rule.  

The numeral один (1) should be used in the proper form to indicate gender (and, number, believe it or not).  For example: один мальчик (one boy), одна девушка (one girl), одно животное (one animal), одни джинсы (one [pair of] jeans).  The plural form of the numeral (1) is a bit of an oddity, but its use will become clear with time.  Following a form of один should come the item at hand in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, as appropriate.

The numerals два (or две), три, and четыре all require the following item to be presented in the genitive singular form.  The numeral два has two forms: два for masculine and neuter items and две for feminine items.  After the numeral два, each numeral has only a single form.  See for example:  две девушки (two girls), три кошки (three cats), четыре мальчика (four boys).  

The numerals пять (5) through двадцать (20) require their attached items in the genitive plural forms, as in these examples:  пять девушек (five girls), десять копеек (ten kopeks), шестнадцать рублей (sixteen rubles).  

Numerals larger than twenty follow the above rules using the last digit.  Think of numerals ending in zero as ending in ten.  For example:  тридцать три рюмки (thirty-three shot glasses), сорок один год (forty-one years), шестьдесят девять слов (sixty-nine words).  

Numerals larger than 99 follow the above rules using the last two digits. For example: двести три собаки (two hundred and three dogs), двести тринадцать собак (two hundred and thirteen dogs).

When the items at hand belong not in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, the numerals themselves must be declined in addition to the declension of the items following them.  This topic shall be discussed here later.  For now, be glad that these forms are relatively uncommon.  Examples follow:  Я видел двух друзей (I saw two friends), Ты не съела ни одной штуки (You didn't eat a single piece).

In short, for nominative (and inanimate accusative) items, use the following rule to select the proper form of the item:

1 -> nominative

2, 3, 4 -> genitive singular

5 and more -> genitive plural

===A few more examples===
* один час
* два часа
* пять часов 
* двадцать один час
* двадцать два часа
shippedText=1 -> nominative

originalSourceText=If the item needs to be in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, use the following rule.  

The numeral один (1) should be used in the proper form to indicate gender (and, number, believe it or not).  For example: один мальчик (one boy), одна девушка (one girl), одно животное (one animal), одни джинсы (one [pair of] jeans).  The plural form of the numeral (1) is a bit of an oddity, but its use will become clear with time.  Following a form of один should come the item at hand in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, as appropriate.

The numerals два (or две), три, and четыре all require the following item to be presented in the genitive singular form.  The numeral два has two forms: два for masculine and neuter items and две for feminine items.  After the numeral два, each numeral has only a single form.  See for example:  две девушки (two girls), три кошки (three cats), четыре мальчика (four boys).  

The numerals пять (5) through двадцать (20) require their attached items in the genitive plural forms, as in these examples:  пять девушек (five girls), десять копеек (ten kopeks), шестнадцать рублей (sixteen rubles).  

Numerals larger than twenty follow the above rules using the last digit.  Think of numerals ending in zero as ending in ten.  For example:  тридцать три рюмки (thirty-three shot glasses), сорок один год (forty-one years), шестьдесят девять слов (sixty-nine words).  

Numerals larger than 99 follow the above rules using the last two digits. For example: двести три собаки (two hundred and three dogs), двести тринадцать собак (two hundred and thirteen dogs).

When the items at hand belong not in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, the numerals themselves must be declined in addition to the declension of the items following them.  This topic shall be discussed here later.  For now, be glad that these forms are relatively uncommon.  Examples follow:  Я видел двух друзей (I saw two friends), Ты не съела ни одной штуки (You didn't eat a single piece).

In short, for nominative (and inanimate accusative) items, use the following rule to select the proper form of the item:

1 -> nominative

2, 3, 4 -> genitive singular

5 and more -> genitive plural

===A few more examples===
* один час
* два часа
* пять часов 
* двадцать один час
* двадцать два часа
shippedText=5 and more -> genitive plural

recordType=example
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Numbers?oldid=4371358
sourceRevision=4371358
sourceRecordId=numbers:If the item needs to be in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, use the 
originalSourceText=If the item needs to be in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, use the following rule.  

The numeral один (1) should be used in the proper form to indicate gender (and, number, believe it or not).  For example: один мальчик (one boy), одна девушка (one girl), одно животное (one animal), одни джинсы (one [pair of] jeans).  The plural form of the numeral (1) is a bit of an oddity, but its use will become clear with time.  Following a form of один should come the item at hand in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, as appropriate.

The numerals два (or две), три, and четыре all require the following item to be presented in the genitive singular form.  The numeral два has two forms: два for masculine and neuter items and две for feminine items.  After the numeral два, each numeral has only a single form.  See for example:  две девушки (two girls), три кошки (three cats), четыре мальчика (four boys).  

The numerals пять (5) through двадцать (20) require their attached items in the genitive plural forms, as in these examples:  пять девушек (five girls), десять копеек (ten kopeks), шестнадцать рублей (sixteen rubles).  

Numerals larger than twenty follow the above rules using the last digit.  Think of numerals ending in zero as ending in ten.  For example:  тридцать три рюмки (thirty-three shot glasses), сорок один год (forty-one years), шестьдесят девять слов (sixty-nine words).  

Numerals larger than 99 follow the above rules using the last two digits. For example: двести три собаки (two hundred and three dogs), двести тринадцать собак (two hundred and thirteen dogs).

When the items at hand belong not in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, the numerals themselves must be declined in addition to the declension of the items following them.  This topic shall be discussed here later.  For now, be glad that these forms are relatively uncommon.  Examples follow:  Я видел двух друзей (I saw two friends), Ты не съела ни одной штуки (You didn't eat a single piece).

In short, for nominative (and inanimate accusative) items, use the following rule to select the proper form of the item:

1 -> nominative

2, 3, 4 -> genitive singular

5 and more -> genitive plural

===A few more examples===
* один час
* два часа
* пять часов 
* двадцать один час
* двадцать два часа
